LAW ENFORCEMENT SPECIALIZED UNITS PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Law Enforcement Specialized Units (LE) Program is to create 
or enhance specialized units that provide a coordinated response to victims of 
domestic violence and their children. 
The specialized units accomplish this through investigation, immediate victim 
advocacy, training for patrol officers and first responders, and the development 
of effective protocols and practices. In addition, each Subrecipient must 
contract with a local Cal OES-funded Domestic Violence Assistance (DV) 
Program Subrecipient for at least one full-time equivalent (FTE) Domestic 
Violence Counselor, pursuant to Evidence Code §1037.1(a)(1). 
The LE Program is supported with funds through the federal 
Services*Training*Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant 
Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161

LAW ENFORCEMENT TRAINING PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Law Enforcement Training (PO) Program is to train law 
enforcement personnel in the areas of sexual assault, domestic violence, 
stalking, and culturally specific dynamics as it relates to these crimes. 
Specific training courses provided include: 
• Domestic Violence for First Responders; 
• Sexual Assault for First Responders; 
• Domestic Violence for Public Safety Dispatchers; 
• Sexual Assault for Public Safety Dispatchers; and 
• Institute of Criminal Investigation (ICI) Domestic Violence for Criminal 
Investigators. 
The PO Program is supported with funds through the federal 
Services*Training*Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant 
Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161

LEGAL ASSISTANCE PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Legal Assistance (XL) Program is to provide holistic civil and 
criminal legal assistance to effectively aid adult and youth victims of crime, 
including those living in rural or isolated communities, who are seeking relief in 
legal matters related to that crime at no cost to the victim. XL Program funds 
may not be used to provide criminal defense services. 
Program components include, but are not limited to: assistance with family law 
matters such as divorce, custody and visitation, and child support; assistance 
with immigration legal matters such as T and U visas; restraining orders; 
employment and tenant rights; and credit repair. 
Organizations that receive XL Program funding are strongly encouraged to 
provide remote access holistic legal assistance to victims of crime who are 
unable to access the business office for reasons including, but not limited to, 
public health orders, victims who live in a rural or remote area, and victims who 
do not have access to safe/reliable transportation. 
The XL Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of 
Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED

VOCA FFY19 $ 5,000,000

LEGAL TRAINING PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Legal Training (LT) Program is to provide consistent quality 
training, publications, and other materials to prosecutors and public defenders 
throughout California. 
Pursuant to California Penal Code §13836, the LT Program provides trainings for 
district attorneys in the investigation and prosecution of sexual assault cases; 
child sexual exploitation cases; child sex abuse cases; and sexual abuse cases 
involving victims with developmental disabilities. The courses include training in 
the unique emotional trauma experienced by victims of these crimes. 
The LT Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Public Prosecutor/Public Defender Training (PPPO), and federal Services* 
Training*Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program 
(STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161, 0385.541

MEDICAL TRAINING CENTER PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Medical Training Center (EM) Program is to ensure forensic 
examinations are conducted in a standard and consistent manner throughout 
California. This is accomplished through the development of standardized forms, 
protocols, and training for forensic medical examiners, law enforcement, 
prosecutors, and court personnel. 
Pursuant to the Penal Code §13823.93, the Medical Training Center (EM) Program 
funds one Subrecipient to provide training for forensic medical examiners in cases 
of sexual assault, child sexual abuse, domestic violence, elder/dependent adult 
abuse, and child physical abuse/neglect. 
The components of the EM Program include, but are not limited to: 
• Maintaining Forensic Medical Evidentiary forms; 
• Developing forensic medical reports used for assault against persons with 
disabilities; 
• Developing and maintaining protocols on how to conduct exams for the 
victim populations listed above; 
• Developing and maintaining protocols on how to conduct examinations for 
assaults against persons with disabilities; 
• Providing standardized, advanced and specialized training for health care 
professionals on how to conduct the exams for the victims populations listed 
above; and 
• Providing technical assistance to sexual assault forensic teams. 
The EM Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds (SGF) – 
Medical Training (MTC0), federal Services*Training* Officers*Prosecutors Violence 
Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP) and federal Victims of Crime Act 
Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.161, 0385.451, 0385.452, 0385.924 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED

MINOR’S COUNSEL PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Minor’s Counsel (MC) Program is to support the courts by 
appointing a minor’s counsel in child custody cases involving child abuse, neglect 
or exploitation, and to determine if the appointment of minor’s counsel improves 
the criminal justice system’s response to these cases. 
The MC Program provides funding to Family Court Services if the judge suspects 
child abuse, neglect, or exploitation has occurred; and when the judge 
determines parents cannot afford to pay the fees. 
The MC Program is supported with federal Children’s Justice Act Grants to States 
Program (CJA0) funds. 
Program/Component: 0385.902 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 CJA0 FFY20 $ 470,000 
FY 2022-23 CJA0 FFY21 $ 470,000 
FY 2023-24 Unknown

NATIVE AMERICAN CHILD ABUSE TREATMENT  PROGRAM  | 2023  
The purpose of the Native American Child Abuse Treatment (NA) Program is to 
provide child abuse treatment services to Native American children victimized 
by abuse and/or neglect. Treatment services include psychotherapy and 
culturally relevant therapy. 
The NA Program also provides supportive services to the non-offending family 
members including resource and referral, assistance to child survivors for the 
participation in the criminal justice system, and crime victim compensation. 
The NA Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of Crime 
Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452

NATIVE AMERICAN DOMESTIC VIOLENCE AND SEXUAL ASSAULT 
PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Native American Domestic Violence and Sexual Assault (DS) 
Program is to provide supportive services to Native American women, and their 
children, who are survivors of sexual assault and domestic violence. Supportive 
services may include: shelter; counseling; court accompaniment; and 
advocacy. 
The DS Program also provides cultural competency trainings to agencies and 
other regional service providers on issues related to Native American women 
survivors of domestic violence and sexual assault. 
The DS Program is supported with funds through federal Services*Training* 
Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161

NATIVE AMERICAN TRIBAL COURT ADVOCATE PROGRAM  | 2023  
The purpose of the Native American Tribal Court Advocate (KT) Program is to 
support tribes as they strengthen their courts. Tribal Court Advocates provide 
trauma-informed, culturally-sensitive victim services, and educate the greater 
tribal community on the process of the tribal court system. 
Tribal Court Advocates are committed to honoring tribal law and governance 
while maintaining the traditional process of respect, representational duty, and 
ethics. Best practices require a collaborative, multi-disciplinary, and culturally-
responsive team to promote community wellness and healing. 
The required components for the KT Program include: 
• Crisis intervention; 
• Case management; 
• Tribal court system assistance; 
• Emergency assistance; and 
• Referrals to existing community resources. 
The KT Program is supported with the following funds: State General Fund (SGF) – 
Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of Crime Act 
Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 SGF-VCGF FY21 $ 292,162 
VOCA FFY20 $ 531,838 
FY 2022-23 VOCA FFY20 $ 549,333 
VOCA FFY21 $ 274,667 
FY 2023-24 Unknown

PAUL COVERDELL FORENSIC SCIENCE IMPROVEMENT ACT PROGRAM | 
2023 
The purpose of the Paul Coverdell Forensic Science Improvement Act (CQ) 
Program is to improve the quality, timeliness, and credibility of forensic science 
services over current operations. 
This is accomplished by providing training for forensic scientists on improving 
laboratory efficiency, courtroom testimony, and by supporting dedicated 
personnel time for reducing backlog and shortening the time for case analysis. 
All crime laboratories must validate their eligibility by certifying their 
accreditation status with an appropriate certifying body such as the American 
Association for Laboratory Accreditation, American National Standards Institute, 
National Accreditation Board, or the National Association of Medical Examiners. 
The CQ Program is supported with funds through the federal Paul Coverdell 
Forensic Science Improvement Act Program (FSIA). 
Program/Component: 0385.523

POSTCONVICTION DNA TESTING ASSISTANCE PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Postconviction DNA Testing Assistance (CK) Program is to 
assist in defraying the costs associated with postconviction DNA testing in cases 
of violent felony offenses in which actual innocence might be demonstrated. 
This is accomplished by utilizing Grant Subaward funds to identify and review 
postconviction cases and to locate and analyze associated biological 
evidence. Samples that previously generated inconclusive DNA results may be 
viable for reanalysis using newer methods. 
The CK Program is supported with funds through the federal Post Conviction 
DNA Testing Assistance Program (DNAE). 
Program/Component: 0385.524 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED

FY 2022-23* N/A

* The FY 2021-22 Grant Subaward performance period is April 1, 2022-September 
30, 2023. No additional funds were allocated for FY 2022-23.

PROBATION OFFICER TRAINING PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Probation Officer Training (PT) Program is to provide in-person 
or online delivery of specialized training to California’s probation officers. The 
training must be victim-centered and focus on enhancing probation officers’ 
knowledge and skills regarding violence against women. Training must focus 
specifically on the supervision of probationers convicted of domestic violence, 
sexual assault, dating violence, and/or stalking. 
The required components of the PT Program include providing statewide 
certified regional trainings on the supervision of probationers convicted of 
domestic violence, sexual assault, dating violence, and/or stalking; providing 
statewide certified regional trainings on best practices in providing 
trauma-informed victim services; increasing access to trainings for probation 
officers; and providing training to California probation officers participating in 
Cal OES’s Probation Specialized Supervision (PU) Program and to other 
California probation officers who request to attend. 
The PT Program is supported with federal Services*Training*Officers*Prosecutors 
Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP) funds. 
Program/Component: 0385.161 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 STOP FFY21 $ 71,000 
FY 2022-23 STOP FFY22 $ 71,000 
FY 2023-24 Unknown 
2021-22 PERFORMANCE STATISTICS 
2 Number of trainings conducted. 
36 Number of individuals trained. 
25 Number of counties represented at trainings.

PROBATION SPECIALIZED UNITS PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Probation Specialized Units (PU) Program is to support 
specialized units within probation departments in California to provide focused 
supervision, through a reduced caseload, to probationers of crime involving 
domestic violence, sexual assault, dating violence, and/or stalking. 
The requirements of the PU Program include a specialized unit of at least one 
probation officer providing continued support to a reduced caseload of 
probationers convicted of a felony crime; the selection of probationers is based 
on the severity of the crime, and supervision techniques are conducted as at 
least 40% face to face contact in the field, 40% face to face contact in the 
office, and no more than 20% of contacts by phone. The probation officer is 
required to monitor counseling/treatment progress; conduct risk assessments; 
review police logs; conduct periodic drug testing; and provide resource and 
referral assistance. Victims are also contacted on a routine basis to inform them 
of the role of the probation officer, terms and conditions of the offender’s 
probation including any changes, and appropriate victim services. 
The PU Program is supported with federal funds through the federal 
Services*Training*Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant 
Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 STOP FFY21 $ 477,263 
FY 2022-23 STOP FFY21 $ 291,650 
STOP FFY22 $ 208,350

RAPE CRISIS PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Rape Crisis (RC) Program is to reduce the level of trauma 
experienced by sexual assault survivors through the provision of comprehensive 
and supportive services, to improve coordination of multi-disciplinary response 
systems, and to expand public awareness. 
Pursuant to California Penal Code §13837 Rape Crisis Centers provide: 
• Crisis intervention, 24 hours per day, seven days per week; 
• Follow-up counseling services; 
• In-person counseling, including group counseling; 
• Accompaniment services; 
• Advocacy services; 
• Information and referrals to victims and the general public; 
• Community education presentations; and 
• Rape prevention presentations and self-defense programs. 
The RC Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Rape Crisis (RCP0), State General Funds (SGF) – Victims of Crime Act 
Supplemental (VCGF), State Penalty Fund (SPF) – Rape Crisis (RCP5), federal 
Sexual Assault Services Formula Program (SASP), federal Services*Training* 
Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP), 
and federal Victims of Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program 
(VOCA), Family Violence Prevention Services and American Rescue Plan (FVSA). 
Program/Component: 0385.154, 0385.161, 0385.301, 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED

SCHOOL RESOURCE OFFICER TRAINING PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the School Resource Officer Training (SO) Program is to train 
K through 12th grade school-based law enforcement officers on fostering 
relationships with youth, identifying the maltreatment of children, providing a 
trauma-informed response to victims of child abuse and neglect, and identifying 
potential resources within the community. 
Required components of the SO Program include, but are not limited to: 
conducting a minimum of 12 trainings across California that is conducted in 
person, no longer than eight hours, held in at least two locations north of 
Sacramento, and geographically located to allow for participation from all 
counties; and developing a robust outreach plan to advertise the training. 
The SO Program is supported with federal Children’s Justice Act Grants to States 
Program (CJA0) funds. 
Program/Component: 0385.902 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 CJA0 FFY19 $ 250,000 
FY 2022-23 CJA0 FFY19 $ 127,031 
CJA0 FFY20 $ 122,969 
FY 2023-24 Unknown 
2021-22 PERFORMANCE STATISTICS 
The training course was finalized and received POST certification on June 21, 2021 
and trainings were held in the following locations: 
• Fremont 
• San Luis Obispo 
• Ontario 
• Orange County 
• Marin 
• Bakersfield 
81 Number of individuals trained.

SEXUAL AND DOMESTIC VIOLENCE PREVENTION PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Sexual and Domestic Violence Prevention (SD) Program is to 
expand sexual and domestic violence prevention efforts in California, prioritize 
marginalized populations within communities, and build the capacity of local 
organizations through funding primary or secondary prevention methodologies. 
This is accomplished by selecting an area of focus (i.e., solely sexual violence; 
solely domestic violence or combined sexual and domestic violence); 
identifying a marginalized population to reach; selecting an existing approved 
prevention strategy and approach to implement; identifying community 
resources and include community stakeholders in the process; customizing 
prevention materials/curriculum to meet the needs of the local sociocultural 
environment; and disseminate information and prevention materials. 
Due to the ongoing COVID-19 pandemic, prevention programs were held 
virtually through Zoom, Google Classroom, and social media platforms, when in-
person training was not an option. 
The SD Program is supported with the following fund: State General Funds (SGF) 
– Domestic Violence and Sexual Assault Prevention (DVSP). These one-time 
funds were allocated through the State Budget in two separate, non-
consecutive years. 
Program/Component: 0385.152 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2019-21 SGF-DVSP FFY19 $ 4,750,000 
FY 2022–24 SGF-DVSP FY21 $ 13,649,999 
FY 2025–26 Unknown 
2020-22 PERFORMANCE STATISTICS 
1,160 Number of events conducted (virtual and in-person). 
4,796 Number of attendees (virtual and in-person).

SEXUAL ASSAULT AMERICAN RESCUE PLAN PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Sexual Assault American Rescue Plan (AR) is to help rape crisis 
centers and sexual assault programs continue adapting to meet the emergency 
needs of sexual assault survivors in response to the COVID-19 public health 
emergency. 
Direct services to victims include, but are not limited to; prevention, preparedness, 
and response support services. These can include: virtual services; supportive 
services; temporary housing; mobile advocacy; training information; and technical 
assistance. All supportive efforts must help reduce the exposure to and risk of 
contracting the COVID-19 virus, assess the needs of sexual assault survivors during 
the COVID-19 public health emergency, and ensure the continuity of sexual 
assault programs and rape crisis center services, culturally specific and tribal 
programs. 
The AR Program is supported with funds through the federal Family Violence 
Prevention and Services Act (FVPSA) American Rescue Plan (ARP) COVID-19 Rape 
Crisis Centers and Sexual Assault Programs (FVSA) Supplemental Funding Program. 
Program Component: 0385.154 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 Program started October 1, 2022 
FY 2022-23 FVSA FFY21 $ 974,992 
FY 2023-24 Unknown Unknown 
2021-22 PERFORMANCE STATISTICS 
The AR Program started October 1, 2022. Statistics will be reported in the next 
JLBC Report. 
AR PROGRAM

SEXUAL ASSAULT AMERICAN RESCUE PLAN TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE 
PROGRAM| 2023 
The purpose of the Sexual Assault American Rescue Plan Technical Assistance 
(AA) Program is to provide training, information, and technical assistance to 
ensure the continuity of sexual assault service providers and rape crisis centers, 
including culturally specific organizations, during the COVID-19 public health 
emergency. 
The AA Program components include, but are not limited to: 
• Conduct assessment to identify training and technical assistance needs; 
• Assess the capacity of the sexual assault service provider and rape crisis 
center, including culturally specific organizations, emergency operation 
plans and their plans to address the needs of survivors and reduce the 
exposure to and risk of contracting the COVID-19 virus; 
• Provide information and referrals as requested; 
• Support victims and survivors of sexual assault to make decisions related to 
their ongoing safety and well-being during the COVID-19 public health 
emergency; 
• Provide individual and group counseling, peer support groups, and referral 
to community-based services to assist sexual assault survivors in recovering 
from the effects of the violence during the COVID-19 health emergency; 
• Provide culturally and linguistically appropriate services during the 
COVID-19 public health emergency. 
The AA Program is supported with the following fund: Federal Family Violence 
Prevention Services and American Rescue Plan (FVSA). 
Program/Component: 0835.154 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-25 FVSA FFY21 $ 379,464 
FY 2025-26 Unknown

SEXUAL ASSAULT LAW ENFORCEMENT SPECIALIZED UNITS PROGRAM| 
2023 
The purpose of the Sexual Assault Law Enforcement Specialized Units (ST) 
Program is to create or enhance specialized units to provide a coordinated 
response to adolescent (age 11 and older) and adult victims of sexual assault. 
The investigator assigned to the specialized unit must meet the following 
requirements: maintain a minimum of half-time assignment to the unit for the 
entire performance period; possess an intermediate Peace Officer Standards 
and Training certificate; possess specialized training in sexual assault 
investigation; review arrest and preliminary investigation reports and conduct 
follow-up interviews; assist with the development and/or revision to response 
protocols for adolescent and adult victims of sexual assault; facilitate training of 
patrol officers/first responders, and other relevant department personnel, on best 
practices; and respond to follow-up or evidence requests from the district 
attorney’s office on assigned adolescent and adult sexual assault cases. 
The ST Program is supported with funds through the federal Services*Training* 
Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING

SEXUAL ASSAULT RESPONSE TEAM PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Sexual Assault Response Team (XS) Program is to enable 
Rape Crisis Program advocates to participate in the planning and coordinating 
of Sexual Assault Response Team (SART) efforts in their local community, with the 
goal of improving the quality of, response to, and services for, sexual assault 
victims. 
Pursuant to Evidence Code §1035.2, the required components of the XS 
Program include committing a minimum of one full-time Sexual Assault 
Counselor to act as a SART Advocate. The SART Advocate must be the lead in 
implementing the following components: developing or enhancing 
representation of multidisciplinary SART; providing training to SART members; and 
providing direct services (first contact/crisis intervention services, follow-up, 
accompaniment, advocacy, and assisting with California Victim Compensation 
Board claims). 
The XS Program is supported with funds through federal Victims of Crime Act 
Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA) and State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) as a supplement for the 
decrease in federal VOCA funding for FFY 2020-21. 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING

SEXUAL ASSAULT TRAINING AND TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE PROGRAM| 
2023 
The purpose of the Sexual Assault Training and Technical Assistance (TE) Program 
is to provide training, technical assistance, resource materials, and networking 
opportunities to Cal OES-funded Rape Crisis Centers (RCCs) and other 
organizations that respond to the needs of sexual assault survivors. 
The TE Program includes, but is not limited to: providing training; providing 
technical assistance; creating or updating resource materials; communicating 
current information related to sexual assault; coordinating sexual assault 
awareness month; and networking. 
The TE Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), State General Funds 
(SGF) – Domestic Violence and Sexual Assault Prevention (DVSP), Federal 
Services*Training* Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant 
Program (STOP); Federal Victims of Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant 
Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.161, 0385.451, 0385,452, 0385.914

SPECIAL NEEDS SCHOOL TRANSPORTATION CAMERA PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Special Needs School Transportation Camera (SN) Program is to 
provide cameras on school transportation used by pre-K though high school 
students with disabilities/special needs to identify abusive behavior that may occur 
between students and/or between students and bus drivers/school personnel. 
Required components of the SN Program include, but are not limited to: engaging 
parents, staff, and students in the discussion of adding cameras to school 
transportation used by pre-K through high school students with disabilities/special 
needs; creating procedures and protocols regarding the use of the camera 
system and the video footage collected; researching, procuring, and installing a 
camera system that is appropriate for the needs identified and coincides with 
written procedures/protocols; and training staff about the camera system and 
keeping parents informed about its use. 
The SN Program is supported with federal Children’s Justice Act Grants to States 
Program (CJA0) funds. 
Program/Component: 0385.902 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22* CJA0 FFY19 $ 23,955 
CJA0 FFY20 $ 175,860 
FY 2022-23 Program ended December 31, 2022.

SPECIALIZED EMERGENCY HOUSING PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Specialized Emergency Housing (KE) Program is to expand 
emergency shelter/emergency housing assistance and provide supportive 
services for victims of crime with specialized needs (e.g., elderly, youth, men, 
disabled, lesbian, gay, bisexual, transgender, queer (LGBTQ+), non-English 
speaking, culturally or religiously marginalized, etc.) who cannot be served 
through a traditional shelter. 
Required components of the KE Program include, but are not limited to: 
emergency shelter/emergency housing assistance; crisis intervention/survivor-
driven advocacy; counseling; emergency food and clothing; transportation; 
permanent housing assistance; financial assistance; and referrals to existing 
community resources. In addition, Subrecipients must address the barriers victims 
experience when accessing housing and supportive services, including lack of 
knowledge about resources, language barriers, social and cultural challenges, 
and accessibility for victims of crime with disabilities. 
The KE Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds (SGF) 
– Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), and Federal Victims of 
Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 SGF-VCGF FY21 $ 3,321,565 
VOCA FFY19 $ 6,051,728 
FY 2022-23 VOCA FFY19 $ 6,400,000 
VOCA FFY21 $ 3,200,000 
FY 2023-24 Unknown

STATE COALITION TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE AND TRAINING PROGRAM 
| 2023 
The purpose of the State Coalition Technical Assistance and Training (BW) 
Program is to provide technical assistance, informational resources, and 
networking opportunities to domestic violence service providers in California. 
This is accomplished through frequent contact with service providers and the 
coordination of an annual conference for the domestic violence field. 
Required components of the BW Program include: 
• Statewide training and technical assistance; 
• Statewide domestic violence conference(s) and issuing travel stipends; 
• Collaboration with Cal OES and the Domestic Violence Advisory Council 
(DVAC); 
• Modeling web-based training curriculum; 
• Resources for Voluntary Participation in Services. 
The BW Program is supported with the following: State General Funds (SGF) – 
Domestic Violence and Sexual Assault Prevention (DVSP), State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), federal Family Violence 
Prevention and Services Grant to States for Domestic Violence Shelters and 
Support Services Program (FVPS), and federal Victims of Crime Act Victim 
Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.151, 0385.451, 0385.452, 0385.914

STATEWIDE CHILD ABUSE TRAINING AND TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE 
PROGRAM | 2023 
The Statewide Child Abuse Training and Technical Assistance (CC) Program 
provides leadership and resources to child abuse treatment centers throughout 
California. Training is provided to multidisciplinary teams, with an emphasis on rural 
communities and communities with a high population of unserved/underserved 
children. 
Additionally, the CC Program is responsible for developing a statewide coalition 
of professionals and agencies that are dedicated to providing effective 
therapeutic services to child victims of abuse, neglect, and other victimizations. 
The CC Program Subrecipient also maintains a directory of statewide service 
providers that can be used for making appropriate referrals and connecting 
children and their caregivers to support services. 
The CC Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds (SGF) 
– Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of Crime Act 
Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452

STATEWIDE DOMESTIC VIOLENCE PREVENTION RESOURCE CENTER 
PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Statewide Domestic Violence Prevention Resource Center 
(PV) Program is to: (a) establish, expand, and maintain a resource center for the 
collection, retention, and distribution of educational materials related to 
domestic violence, family violence, and/or teen dating violence and its 
prevention, (b) prevent intimate partner violence, including teen dating 
violence, prioritize underserved populations within communities, and build the 
capacity of local organizations to do this work, and (c) provide training and 
ongoing technical assistance for Cal OES Intimate Partner Violence Prevention 
(FD) Programs. 
Required components of the PV Program include: 
• Employing experienced Domestic Violence, Intimate Partner Violence 
prevention staff; 
• Providing skills-based training; 
• Networking; 
• Providing technical assistance; and 
• Maintaining a resource center. 
The PV Program is supported with the following: State General Funds (SGF) – 
Family Violence Prevention (FV00), federal Family Violence Prevention and 
Services Grant to States for Domestic Violence Shelters and Support Services 
Program (FVPS), and federal Services*Training*Officers*Prosecutors Violence 
Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.151, 0385.152, 0385.161

STATEWIDE PREVENTION COORDINATION PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Statewide Prevention Coordination (PP) Program is to 
facilitate statewide coordination of domestic violence (DV) and sexual violence 
(SV) prevention. The PP Program Subrecipient also provides collaborative 
support to Cal OES on the development and implementation of DV and SV 
prevention programs and provides training and technical assistance to Cal OES 
Subrecipients under the Sexual and Domestic Violence Prevention (SD) Program. 
The required components for the PP Program include, but are not limited to: 
coordinating statewide prevention efforts; collaborating between partner 
agencies (including the California Department of Public Health, the California 
Partnership to End Domestic Violence, ValorUS, and Cal OES); providing 
technical assistance to Cal OES TV and SD Program Subrecipients; and 
evaluating TV and SD Programs. 
The PP Program is supported with the following: and State General Funds 
(SGF) – Domestic Violence and Sexual Assault Prevention (DVSP), State General 
Funds (SGF) – Family Violence Prevention (FV00), and federal Services*Training* 
Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.152, 0385.161, 0385.914

SUPERVISED VISITATION CENTERS PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Supervised Visitation Centers (SP) Program is to assure the 
safety and protection of children by supporting professional providers who 
supervise visitation of children with a noncustodial party where there is a history 
or allegations of domestic violence, child abuse or neglect, or sexual abuse. 
Subrecipients must be a local public agency or nonprofit entity that is a 
subcontractor of a court for the purposes of supervised visitation and that satisfy 
the Uniform Standards of Practice for Providers of Supervised Visitation. Required 
components of the supervised visitation include on-site, one-to-one, or group 
supervision to provide the visiting parent/caregiver a chance to address specific 
issues; to help reintroduce a parent and child after a long absence; to introduce 
a parent and child where there has been no existing relationship; when there is 
a history of domestic violence, child abuse or neglect, or sexual abuse; when 
there are parenting concerns or mental illness; or when there is a parental threat 
of abduction. 
The SP Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of 
Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 SGF-VCGF FY21 $ 929,950 
VOCA FFY20 $ 1,692,838

FY 2023-24 Unknown

for funding in February 2023.

TEEN DATING  VIOLENCE PREVENTION PROGRAM  | 2023  
The purpose of the Teen Dating Violence Prevention (TV) Program was to expand 
p
revention efforts in California and build the capacity of local prevention efforts 
in schools by funding organizations to implement, maintain, and evaluate 
comprehensive, school-based, teen dating violence prevention programs that 
address school climate within their service area. 
The required components of the TV Program included, but were not limited to: 
• Maintaining Operational Agreement(s) with selected school(s); 
• Staffing one full-time equivalent Prevention Coordinator; 
• Providing skills-based training; 
• Networking; 
• Conducting site visits with the California Department of Public Health; 
• Conducting a School Climate Survey; 
• Conducting trainings for school staff and administrators; 
• Supporting a youth leadership team; 
• Assisting with strengthening and maintaining the School Teen Dating 
Violence (TDV) Prevention Policy; 
• Implementing school-based curriculum in a classroom setting; 
• Conducting a School-Wide TDV Prevention Campaign; and 
• Providing stipends to participating schools. 
The TV Program was supported by the federal Services*Training*Officers* 
Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161

TRANSITIONAL HOUSING PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Transitional Housing (XH) Program is to provide victims of 
crime with transitional/short-term housing assistance and a range of supportive 
services with the goal of moving victims into safe, permanent housing. 
The XH Program is designed after the Housing First Model, as defined in Welfare 
and Institutions (W&I) Code Section 8255 to address the prevalence of 
homelessness in California. The Housing First Model is an approach to connect 
individuals and families experiencing homelessness quickly and successfully to 
permanent housing without preconditions and barriers to entry, such as sobriety, 
treatment, or service participation requirements. 
Required components of the XH Program include, but are not limited to: 
transitional/short-term housing assistance; community engagement; and 
supportive services. Organizations that receive XH Program funding must also 
hire a one half-time equivalent advocate in the role of Housing Coordinator. 
The XH Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), and federal Victims of 
Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 SGF-VCGF FY21 $ 6,101,173 
VOCA FFY19 $ 8,600,000 
VOCA FFY20 $ 2,506,296

VOCA FFY21 $ 9,490,943 
FY 2023-24 Unknown

TRAUMA INFORMED CARE & BRAIN SCIENCE PRINCIPLES TRAINING 
PROGRAM| 2023 
The purpose of the Trauma Informed Care & Brain Science Principles 
Training (TQ) Program was to provide comprehensive web-based training on 
trauma-informed approaches and brain science principles to various 
professional disciplines that handle child abuse cases or work with children who 
have been traumatized by abuse. The training is focused on reducing trauma to 
victims of child abuse and their families. 
Training covered the effects of trauma on children, how children’s brains are 
affected by trauma, and how to effectively work with children who have 
experienced trauma. The training includedd innovative trauma-informed 
approaches, nationally-recognized studies on trauma and brain science, 
examples and case studies specific to each training audience, practical tools to 
mitigate stress and increase resilience, and cultural competency. 
The TQ Program was supported with funds through the federal Children's Justice 
Act Grants to States Program (CJA0). 
Program/Component: 0385.902 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 Program ended September 30, 2021 
FY 2022-23 Unknown 
FY 2-23-24 Unknown 
*This program is included as the final three months of the Grant Subaward 
performance period occurred in FY 2021-22. 
2021-22 PERFORMANCE STATISTICS 
The training has been completed and is available online. Modules include the 
following: 
• Overview: The Effects of Trauma on the Brain and Behavior; 
• Historical: The Influence of Community Violence and Culture; 
• Impact: The Long-lasting Effects of Trauma, featuring voices of survivors 
and caregivers; and 
• Discipline Specific: Legal, Medical, Victim Advocates, Child Protective 
Services, Law Enforcement, Caregivers, Educators, Public Health. 
Each module includes a quiz to help measure the learners’ knowledge before 
and after completion of the modules. 
Continuing education credit is available for Marriage and Family Therapists and 
Licensed Clinical Social Workers. 
TQ PROGRAM

TRIBAL CRISIS RESPONSE TRAINING PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Tribal Crisis Response Training (CH) Program is to provide 
comprehensive crisis response, and train tribes and tribal organizations in 
California on crisis response. The CH Program delivers crisis intervention services 
and evaluates traumatic events in Indian Country. 
The goal of the CH Program is to build a network of highly trained tribal 
professionals coordinated through a single system, who can respond in a 
comprehensive and timely manner to the needs of survivors of disaster and/or 
traumatic events and to provide no-cost crisis response, follow-up consultation, 
and referrals. 
The CH Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) - Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of Crime 
Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 SGF-VCGF FY21 $ 35,457 
VOCA FFY20 $ 64,543 
FY 2022-23 SCF-VCGF FY21 $ 50,000 
VOCA FFY22 $ 50,000 
FY 2023-24 Unknown

UNSERVED/UNDERSERVED CHILD & YOUTH ADVOCACY PROGRAM | 
2023 
The purpose of the Unserved/Underserved Child and Youth Advocacy (XY) 
Program is to provide funding for advocacy and support services to 
unserved/underserved child and youth survivors of crime, by maintaining and/or 
enhancing existing programs. The unserved/underserved population is 
characterized as any victim population that disproportionately accesses victim 
services in the agency’s service area. 
This is accomplished by agencies hiring staff that reflect the 
unserved/underserved population, training all staff on the cultural norms of the 
population, and increasing outreach efforts. 
The XY Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds (SGF) – 
Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of Crime Act 
Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED

VOCA FFY20 $ 2,100,530 
FY 2022-23 VOCA FFY20 $ 1,627,000 
VOCA FFY22 $ 1,627,000 
FY 2023-24 Unknown

UNSERVED/UNDERSERVED VICTIM ADVOCACY AND OUTREACH | 2023 
The purpose of the Unserved/Underserved Victim Advocacy and Outreach (UV) 
Program is to increase access to culturally appropriate victim services for 
unserved/underserved survivors of crime. 
The program components for the UV Program include the following services: 
• Crisis intervention; 
• Counseling; 
• Outreach; 
• Emergency Financial Assistance; 
• Criminal Justice Support And Advocacy; 
• Staffing; 
• Training; 
• Assistance with California Victim Compensation Board claims. 
The UV Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF) and federal Victims of 
Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING ALLOCATED

VICTIM ADVOCACY IN DETENTION FACILITIES PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Victim Advocacy in Detention Facilities (KA) Program is to 
increase access to appropriate services for victims of sexual assault in local 
detention facilities, including adult detention facilities, juvenile detention 
facilities, and immigration detention facilities. 
This is accomplished by funding a Sexual Assault Counselor, per Evidence Code 
§1035.2, to focus solely on serving victims in detention facilities and acting as the 
point of contact to assist in eliminating physical or programmatic barriers. 
Required components of the KA Program include: collaborating with detention 
facilities; assisting with California Victim Compensation Board Claims; providing 
direct services; and participating in the Sexual Assault Team meetings. 
The KA Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), federal Victims of 
Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA), and Family 
Violence Prevention Services and American Rescue Plan (FVSA). 
Program/Component: 0385.154, 0385.451, 0385.452

VICTIM INFORMATION AND NOTIFICATION EVERYDAY PROGRAM | 
2023 
The purpose of the Victim Information and Notification Everyday (VN) Program is to 
track the location of inmates while incarcerated and inform victims of changes to 
incarceration location and/or release. 
The VN Program, also known as VINE, supports the National Victim Notification 
Network services providing access to timely and reliable information about criminal 
cases and the custody status of offenders, 24-hours per day. This is accomplished 
by providing crime victims with VINE tracking service access; recording direct 
services; providing services regardless of immigration status; and providing free 
notification services. Victims and other concerned citizens can register by phone, 
email, text message, or TTY device to be notified whenever an offender’s custody 
status changes. The system communicates with jail and prison booking systems in 
near real-time by transmitting updated information to the Appriss Data Network, 
which manages the automated interfaces and monitors offender status. VINE 
utilizes a free downloadable app via cellphone, laptop, or computer. When a 
change in the custody status of an offender occurs, county jails and state prison 
facilities transmit information through Appriss, which then immediately notifies 
registered crime victims of the offender’s new status. 
The VN Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds (SGF) – 
Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), State Penalty Fund (SPF) – Victim-
Witness Assistance (VWAO), State General Funds (SGF) – Victim Information and 
Notification Everyday (VINE), and federal Victims of Crime Act Victim Assistance 
Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.103, 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING 
ALLOCATED 
FY 2021-22 SGF-VCGF FY21 $ 410,670 
SPF-VWA0 FY21 $ 739,000 
VOCA FFY19 $ 747,545

VICTIM NOTIFICATION PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Victim Notification (VT) Program is to provide direct 
assistance to victims of violent crime, assist victims as soon as possible after the 
crime occurs to reduce the severity of the psychological consequences of the 
victimization, and assist the victim in navigating the criminal justice appeals 
system. 
This is accomplished by utilizing a system created by the VT Program 
Subrecipient whereby crime victims are provided with timely information about 
a defendant’s appeal on capital and non-capital cases. 
The VT Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), and federal Victims of 
Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program funds (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING

VICTIM SERVICES INFORMATION PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Victim Services Information (VI) Program is to support the 
California Department of Corrections and Rehabilitation to provide direct victim 
services in a correctional setting. 
The VI Program responds to various needs of victims and ensures inmates, 
parolees, correctional staff, and the public recognize and understand the 
impact of crime on victims. Victim services provided by the project include 
notifying victims of the whereabouts of offenders and circumstances of the 
case, counseling, educating regarding victim’s rights, and assisting with the 
preparation of victim impact statements. 
The VI Program is supported with the following funds: federal Victims of Crime 
Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA), and State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF). 
Program/Component: 0385.451, 0385.452

VICTIM WITNESS ASSISTANCE PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Victim Witness Assistance (VW) Program is to maintain Victim 
Witness Assistance Centers (Centers), in each of California’s 58 counties, to 
provide comprehensive services to victims and witnesses of all types of violent 
crime, pursuant to California Penal Code §13835. 
The required components of the VW Program include, but are not limited to: 
• Crisis intervention; 
• Emergency assistance; 
• Orientation to the criminal justice system; 
• Court escort; 
• Case status/disposition; 
• Victim compensation claims assistance; 
• Direct counseling; property return; 
• Notification of family, friends, and employers; 
• Resource and referral assistance; 
• Restitution assistance (upon request of the victim); 
• Outreach; 
• Public presentations; and 
• Training for criminal justice agencies. 
Centers are located in district attorney’s offices, probation departments, county 
sheriff’s offices, and non-governmental organizations. The VW Program also 
provides for a mass victimization advocate (MVA) position to develop and 
implement a victim assistance plan to support and enhance immediate 
response and recovery efforts and establish readiness in response to criminal 
mass victimization/terrorism incidents. 
The VW Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), State Penalty Fund 
(SPF) – Victim-Witness Assistance (VWA0), federal Services*Training* Officers* 
Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP), and 
federal Victims of Crime Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.101, 0385.161, 0385.451, 0385.452 
VW PROGRAM

VICTIMS LEGAL RESOURCE CENTER PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Victims Legal Resource Center (VR) Program is to provide 
victims, their families, and victim service providers with information about victims’ 
legal rights in the criminal and civil justice systems, as well as tailored resource 
referrals. 
The VR Program was established through the passage of Assembly Bill 1176 and 
codified as California Penal Codes §13897-13897.3. The intent of the legislation 
was to ensure that crime victims receive much needed information regarding 
legal issues. Required components of the VR Program include, but are not 
limited to: expanded hours of operation of the California Victim Resource 
Center to increase access to legal information, referral, and resources; providing 
callers with information about victims’ legal rights to compensation; providing 
service providers with information regarding the legal rights of victims of crime; 
advising callers about potential civil causes of action and references to local 
legal aid and lawyer referral services; advising and assisting callers in 
understanding and implementing their rights to participate in sentencing and 
parole eligibility hearings; advising callers about victims’ rights in the criminal 
system, assisting them in overcoming problems, assisting with the return of 
property, and informing them of procedures that protect witnesses; referring 
callers to local programs which include victim-witness programs, rape crisis units, 
domestic violence programs, and child sexual abuse centers; and referring 
callers to local resources for information about public and private benefits and 
the means of obtaining aid. 
The VR Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims Legal Resource Center (VLRC), State General Funds 
(SGF) – Victims of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), and federal Victims of Crime 
Act Victim Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.102, 0385.451, 0385.452

VIOLENCE AGAINST WOMEN VERTICAL PROSECUTION PROGRAM | 
2023 
The purpose of the Violence Against Women Vertical Prosecution (VV) Program 
is to improve the criminal justice system’s response to violent crimes against 
women through a coordinated, multi-disciplinary, prosecutorial response 
including specialized units with a highly qualified prosecutor and victim 
advocate. 
The required components of the VV Program include vertical prosecution, when 
a prosecutor and a victim advocate are assigned to a case from the initial filing 
of charges through the penalty phase; victim advocacy; only highly qualified 
staff are assigned to the vertical prosecution unit; and staff must attend regularly 
scheduled multidisciplinary meetings in the project service area that pertains to 
the crime type(s) selected for vertical prosecution. Vertical prosecution has 
shown to improve conviction rates, reduce victim trauma, and provide more 
consistent and appropriate sentencing. 
The VV Program is supported with funds through federal Services*Training* 
Officers*Prosecutors Violence Against Women Formula Grant Program (STOP). 
Program/Component: 0385.161 
FISCAL YEAR FUND SOURCE(S) FUNDING

YOUTH EMERGENCY TELEPHONE REFERRAL NETWORK PROGRAM | 2023 
The purpose of the Youth Emergency Telephone Referral Network (YT) Program is to 
provide a non-threatening, toll-free hotline to connect youth in need of services to 
appropriate service agencies. The referral can be accessed by youth and their 
families from anywhere in the State. 
Hotline counselors assist callers via the phone line, text, or online chat. They counsel 
callers and connect them to appropriate service providers, providing a warm hand-
off and terminating participation in the call once a connection has been made. The 
hotline can also be used as a message center for parents, guardians, and/or youth 
who wish to communicate with each other but do not want to speak directly. 
The YT Program is supported with the following funds: State General Funds (SGF) – 
Youth Emergency Telephone Referral (YET0), State General Funds (SGF) – Victims 
of Crime Act Supplemental (VCGF), and federal Victims of Crime Act Victim 
Assistance Formula Grant Program (VOCA). 
Program/Component: 0385.352, 0385.451, 0385.452
